win

SUGGESTED TECHNIQUES FOR HYPNOSIS AND THERAPY OF ABDUCTEES
C. 1992 SES ee

INTRUDERS FOUNDATION
P.O. Box 30233
New York, NY 10011

INTRODUCTION

This paper is intended for researchers and therapists who
wish to do regressive hypnosis and treatment of abductees. It is
assumed that the researchers and therapists are familiar with the
literature of abductions especially Secret Life by David M.
Jacobs. and Intruders and Missing Time by Budd Hopkins.

Both UFO researchers and therapists throughout the United
States have increasingly turned their attention to the abduction
phenomenon. A 1991 Roper Poll survey indicated that at least two
percent of Americans have had experiences consistent with those
that known abductees have had. Because of the magnitude of the
phenomenon, standardization of methodology has become essential
in the treatment of abduction victims and in the investigation of
abduction cases so that the full scope of the problem can be
understood clearly. Therapists have had little guidance in .
treating abductees and are mainly unaware of the problems that
abductions can engender in their clients. Similarly.
investigators have often delved into this troubling area with

‘little knowledge of the difficulties in understanding what has

happened to the abductees or of the psychological tconsequences of
their investigations for the abductee. This paper is a brief
survey of some of the main problems in hypnosis and therapy that
the authors have encountered in their research.

THE INITIAL INTERVIEW

In general abductees do not remember or understand what has
happened to them. Within seconds after the abduction the
majority of individuals forget the events. Even though they know
that they might have had a series of very unusual experiences
over the course of their lives. and they may even suspect that
they are involved with the abduction phenomenon, they still do

not know exactly what has happened to them. They may remember
bits and pieces of an abduction but cannot put it all together.
Some remember a large part of the experience, while others
remember absolutely nothing. The most central thing that people
Want to know is exactly what has happened to them. Some seek
reassurance that they are “normal” when all their life they have
felt that the bizarre things happening to them must be a result
of mental aberration. Others feel that they would rather be
mentally ill than have to endure the overwhelming trauma of being
abducted. Mental illness can be treated, they feel, but they are

completely powerless in the face of abductions.

When abduction events are remembered consciously,
circumstances are much easier for the researcher. The problems
inherent in hypnosis are eliminated and the memories can have a
more tangible reality for the abductee. Unfortunately.
experience has shown that consciously recalled events can be
notoriously inaccurate and unreliable. Hypnosis properly used--
asking the right questions in the right way at the right time--
ean elicit information that surpasses consciously recalled
material in its detail and accuracy. In fact, fragments of
conscious memory often lead people into the belief that other
things have happened to them rather than abductions. Depending
upon their previous belief systems they might think that they
have had unexpected out of body experiences, seen ghosts, had
religious visitations, traveled on the “astral plane.” seen the
devil. and so forth. No matter how they have been interpreted,
all of these experiences are worthy of investigation. Just as
often, the client might remember having inexplicable missing time
experiences, seeing strange lights in their rooms, seeing a
“monster’ in their room, having vivid or lifelike “dreams” about
aliens. UFOs, or bizarre babies. and so forth. Asking a series
of questions about unusual events in one’s life can elicit many
conscious memories which might be related to abduction events.
Each of these incidents provides an excellent target area for the
researcher to investigate through hypnosis. It is usually better
to look into specific events than to randomly search for them
because the targeted event is often one that the client has
consciously remembered as having emotional significance for him.

During initial and subsequent interviews before hypnosis. it
is extremely advisable for the investigator to behave in a
supportive and accepting manner. If one is skeptical or hostile
to the idea of abductions, the abductee will most likely not wish
to be involved with that researcher or therapist. This does not
mean that one has to label the client as an abductee immediately.
If pushed, the best policy is to tell the client that although a
certain event might be “suspicious,” even a seasoned researcher
cannot tell exactly what has happened unless it is investigated.
7 3

FIRST-TIME HYPNOSIS AND THRESHOLD ANXIETY.

The first time a person comes to explore his unusual events
ean be the most difficult. The subject comes to the house or
office of a person whom he might never have met before. He is to
undergo a mysterious form of mind alteration called hypnosis. He
fears that he might remember frightening and traumatic memories
of being abducted by aliens. He is afraid that he will be out of
control and blurt out the intimate details of his personal life.
He is afraid that the hypnotist will make him say or do things
against his will. It is hard to imagine a more fear~engendering
therapeutic situation than this. Therefore, the
researcher/therapist should not expect to have total success in
recalling an abduction the first few times that the subject
attempts to do regressive hypnosis. Sometimes memories are
ambiguous and unclear. People simply forget events, no matter
how they are recalled; hypnosis is not a magic key to instantly

unlock all that has been stored.

Often the clients’ experiences are so bizarre that. instead
of saying anything that comes into their minds, abductees are ;
often quite concerned about sounding foolish. Consequently, they
censor themselves, remaining silent about meaningful things
happening to them. ‘The researcher/therapist should instruct the ©
subject to report everything that he is ,seeing and feeling.
regardless of how bizarre it sounds. “We can sort it out later”
is one way aescribe the process to the client.

During the hypnosis session, anxiety tends to rise just
before an abduction procedure begins. Once it begins. the
anxiety level tends to drop off only to rise again as the next
“procedure is about to occur. The effect is like an emotional
roller coaster ride. Explain this to the subject before the
first hypnosis session so that he will be aware of that
possibility and will be prepared for it. If the fear during the
session is too great, try to lessen it through the hypnotic
technique of physically changing the abductee’s perspective of
the scene being remembered in order to "distance" him from it.

THE HYPNOSIS SESSION-- INDUCTION

A relaxation induction is advisable because it allows the
subject to feel calm while remembering extraordinary and
frightening events. Because of the uniqueness of the phenomenon,
induction metaphors must be chosen carefully so that the subject
is not frightened by inappropriate images. Avoid using phrases
like "You will feel that you are floating” or “You will see a
bright light and be drawn to it.” Any phrases that might give
: , 4.

the impression of being out of control, flying. floating, or
being within lights might generate anxiety. Hopkins and Jacobs
use a muscle relaxation induction with a deepening process that
requires a person to go to a beautiful beach or other pleasant
environment. You might wish to use the beach or other area (be
careful it is not an area in which the abduction took place} as a
“safe haven” in which to bring the abductee if fear during the

Session becomes too great.

Once the induction is completed, it is useful for the
abductee go back to the events just prior to the abduction--
driving in a car, getting ready to go to bed, and so forth. This
accustoms the subject to hypnosis and demonstrates his complete
intellectual control: He can say or think whatever he wishes.

In addition to lessening anxiety, this process also allows for
the person to deepen the hypnotic state by becoming naturally

more relaxed within it.

BLOCKS

In the first few hypnotic sessions. it sometimes becomes
difficult to move the subject through the abduction. When this
happens, a variety of techniques can be used to unblock the
subject. Peeking behind a closed curtain at the count of three
to discover an event or memory, seeing the abduction on a movie
screen, counting from ten to one to have a memory form and come
to consciousness-- all are helpful. Most hypnotherapists are
familiar with a number of techniques well-suited for this work.
Patience is required with blocks--especially when the memories

are traumatic. 1

THE STRUCTURE OF THE ABDUCTION

In our experience with the reports, every abduction seems to
have a specific purpose, and it usually is not just the physical.
examination. It is incumbent upon the therapist/and investigator
to discern what that purpose is. If an adult is recounting an
abduction event and the only thing that he remembers is the
examination, then one is virtually assured that other, more
significant. procedures are being skipped. The purpose of the
abduction might be to collect sperm and ova, to extract a fetus,
te conduct a child-presentation procedure, to observe human
‘emotional reactions, and so forth. It is rare that the purpose
ef the abduction is simply the examination. Generally the
reasons seem to fall within the confines of the systematic
abduction matrix as described by Jacobs in Secret Life (Appendix
5

C). It is important to remember, however, that the matrix is of
commonly seen abduction procedures and there are many other
procedures that do not have a place on the matrix. The more that
is learned about the phenomenon, the more it is likely that the
Matrix will be further developed and expanded.

Sometimes an investigator will have a specific idiosyncratic
interpretation in mind for the abduction phenomenon. Although
the abductee might agree with it, the investigator must be
careful not to impose this structure on the subject. It is
absolutely essential that the interpretation of abduction events
should be generated from the clearly and competently developed
accounts and not from an investigator’s private scenario.

BECOMING ACCUSTOMED TO RELATING THE ACCOUNT

Exploring the very beginning of the abduction event can be
the most difficult part of the session. Patience is reguired.
Along with the trauma of the event. the abductee almost always
experiences a consciousness and perceptual alteration that makes
it difficult to clearly relate his experiences, especially in the
beginning. Blocks most often occur at the beginning phase of the
abduction because this is also when the fear is usually most
profound. For example, if the subject wakes up paralyzed in the
middle of the night with the sense of a presence in the room, the
questioning should be calm and conversational. It is helpful to
ask where, spatially, the presence might be; on the left, the
right, the foot of the bed, and so forth. Ask if the room is
dark. normally lighted. or if there are any lighting changes.

Try to situate the subject spatially so that he begins to think
more about the physical surroundings rather than just his own
predicament. Ask if the subject senses or sees just the one
presence. Ask what that presence is doing, where he is facing,
if he is moving, and the like. Ask the same questions about the
abductee’s movements and position. Usually the fear level is
very high, but it is important to keep the subject moving forward
rather than focussing on the fear, which usually lessens after -

the abduction begins.

Sometimes the abductee’s memories begin in the middle of an
abduction. Carefully moving the subject backward in time is a :
good solution to uncovering the chronology of events. This is
also a workable technique for recovering memories recalled as
“dreams.” If the subject is lying on a table, move him back just
a bit to see if he can remember getting up on the table, move
back a bit more to remember moving toward the table, move back
more to when he entered into the room with the table. and so

forth.

Once the abduction has begun, ideally, the person will
describe moving from his or her normal environment into the
object in which the abduction procedures will take place. An
excellent way to conduct the interview is to move forward from
one moment to the next until the entire abduction sequence has
been carried through. This helps to insure that the events will

be remembered in a chronological, coherent form.

POSITIONING, FRAMING, AND KEY QUESTIONS

Positioning questions allow the subject to grow accustomed
to reporting what he or she is doing without focussing on
specific procedures being administered to him. These are
“deflecting” questions that allow the subject to assume the role
of participant/observer rather than victim. Some sample
positioning questions are: “Are you in a large room, small room,
medium sized room?" “What. is the lighting like in the room--is
it dim. bright, somewhere in the middle?“ "What is your own
_posture--are you standing, sitting, lying down, crouching. etc.?"
Where is the position of your arms?,” etc. It is sometimes
appropriate to ask these questions when anxiety rises during the
session 50 that the. abductee can focus on other events instead of
the terror engendered in the situation. After the subject is
calmed, and the researcher/therapist recapitulates his situation,
it is advisable to go to the basic framing question: “And what
happens next?" Whatever the response is, it is of the utmost
importance for the abductee to lead the hypnotist into his or her

own experiences.

The framing question allows the abductee to move forward and
it defines the direction of the next series of questions for the
researcher and therapist. After the framing question has been
responded to it might be necessary to ask a key question. Key
questions usually help to uncover skipped material or deeply
buried material that the abductee has trouble bringing to normal
memory. In general it is advisable to ask questions about
material already stated. When, in response to a framing
question, the abductee says "I don’t know," restating the
situation helps: “Let’s see, you're standing in the middle of
the room, you see a table in front of you, what do the beings do
then?" This helps the abductee to move forward. It is also
necessary to ask questions about apparent contradictions: “A few
minutes ago you said that the room was immense, as big as an
airplane hanger, yet you can touch two walls with your arms, I
don’t understand.“ This helps the abductee to understand the
consciousness alteration that most of them go through and it also
allows them to be very careful in their remembrances.

It is also necessary to ask purposefully leading questions
from time to time to gauge whether the abductee can be led. The
questions should be leading into an answer that the researcher
knows to be false. For example, asking whether the room the
abductee is in is square with a flat ceiling and carpenter’s
corners is useful. Asking whether the aliens have flat, broad,
Chins is another disconfirming leading question. In general,
abductees refuse to be led and answer with a firm "no" to these
questions. If an abductee can be led, the therapist\investigator
must be extremely careful not to ask ieading questions,
purposefully or otherwise, in the session.

MOVING FORWARD AND SKIPS

During the course of relating the abduction chronology, it
is quite common for abductees to skip over some of the most
traumatic and affective material. They do this because of fear,
embarrassment, or because the events are so bizarre that they
have difficulty assimilating them into a normal reality. They
_ might skip over the passage from their normal environment to the

UFO, or getting their clothes off just before the examination.
Sometimes the entire examination is skipped. Budd Hopkins uses
an extremely successful Body Inventory technique for this phase
of.the abduction. He tells the abductee that his body retains a
sensory memory of things that have happened to it during the
abduction. The subject’s body will be able to remember the touch
that it felt even if his eyes were closed (this mitigates fear).
In general, abductees remember having their eyes open but this
technique allows them to relate the full examination procedures
without having to engage with it visually. The hypnotist then
mentions areas of the body, beginning with the feet and moving
systematically to the head. He asks if each area (feet, lower
legs, thighs, genitals, lower abdomen, stomach and chest, arms
and hands, head, and the facial features) feels any different
sensations (pleasure, pain, an itch, a tickle, heat, cold.
tension. relaxation, numbness, sensitivity, and so on) or does
each part feel perfectly normal. A suggestion later that they
can open their eyes a bit and “peek” is often helpful.

Many skips are not very important for therapy and research,
but there are some more significant areas to know about. After
the examination is over, when the framing question “What happens
next?" is asked, the person is liable to say, “I’m back in ny
bed." While this will eventually be true, it is not likely at
that time. Because every abduction has a purpose, and the
purpose is usually not simply the examination, it is most likely
that critical procedures have been skipped. Thus, the researcher
should say, “Let me back you up a bit. The exam is over'and you
: , 8

are lying there, Does anybody come over to you?" This is a key
question, leading to a point. It could mean that the being is
only about to take the abductee off the table, but there are
other possibilities. To be certain, here are a few general —

questions and answers.

At this point the abductee is in a "Mindscan” situation--a
taller being stares deeply into the eyes of an abductee
presumably either to extract data, to examine emotions, or to
elicit emotions. This procedure can be quite shattering and must

be confronted squarely by the therapist. It is crucial to spend
some time on this situation because of its usually negative

influence on the subject.

Q: Why don’t you just close your eyes?

A: No.

“How do you feel about him while this

This is a “Bonding” procedure in which feelings of love,
attachment, and attraction are elicited from the abductees toward
the being who is performing the procedure. Sometimes abductees
talk about a strong sexual aspect to these emotions and they also
remember procedures of a sexual nature being performed upon them.
The later consequences of these experiences on an abductee’s
Psychological well-being can be extremely significant and the
therapist should confront these feelings squarely, allowing the
subject to understand that the emotions have been purposefully
elicited: he or she must not feel guilt or shame. After the
session, the possible long-term influences of the bonding and
Sexual procedures on his or her life can be discussed.

Immediately after the taller being finishes the Mindscan
Procedure, he usually begins his gynecological or urological
procedures. The key question in this situation is: "Where does
he go after he stares at you?“ The usual answer is that he goes
down to the foot of the table. The next question might be a
Position question: “What is the position of your legs?" The
answer most often is that their knees are up and legs spread.
From that point the next framing question is "What does he do
next?" or, "What happens next?” and the answer is that he is
performing a gynecological procedure. Questions can be asked
about this procedure to determine exactly what is happening to
the person, tissue sample, ova collection, fetal implantation,
fetal extraction. Men often have a taller female being stare
into their eyes. During that time a device may be placed on
their genitals and sperm extracted. Sometimes this procedure is
accompanied by sexual imagery so that the male abductee thinks
that he is about to have a sexual encounter with a beautiful

woman.

or

After the gynecological and urological procedures, it is

possible that a variety of other experiences might take place.

If the abductee says that after these procedures he or she is now
getting dressed in preparation to go back, that might be exactly
what is happening, and in fact that is very often the end of it.
However, it is possible that something else also happened. The
key question is “After the examination is completed, do they show
you anything?" This is, of course, a leading question, but it is
so broad that virtually anything can be inserted into the answer.
Often the beings show the abductee a small box that she must look
at while one stands next to her and stares into her eyes.
Frequently an abductee is taken into another room and shown an
infant or small child. The child-presentation procedures usually
involve the abductees holding, hugging, or playing with one or
more of the offspring. The female abductee may be asked to nurse
a small baby, and when she does she may find that she’s
lactating, a process that has been somehow stimulated during the
examination phase of the abduction. When the offspring is taken
away the subject may experience a great amount of sorrow and °
guilt. a reaction which must be addressed both during and after

the session.
10

From time to time the abductee will be given mental
procedures to endure. These consist of visualization procedures
which require viewing images on a screen, envisioning images
placed in his mind, participating in an interactive staged and
envisioned event, or performing some task. Researching the first
three procedures includes asking a key question: "What is the
being doing who is closest to you?" The answer is. usually that
the being is looking inte the abductee’s eyes in a staring
procedure closely akin to Mindscan. The reports indicate that
the alien might be examining the abductee’s emotional reactions
to the procedure for reasons unknown. I+ is important for the
therapist to understand this because observing images or
participating in staged events might be quite frightening or
anxiety-provoking, and learning that a mental examination is at
the heart of this event allows for the abductee to gain :
perspective on the procedure after it is described. Staging
procedures, in which abductees are made to "act out" a
prearranged scenario, are sometimes difficult to recognize, but
patience and experience can unlock the complex of activities that
these puzzling events present. Testing is something that is less
well understood. It involves a sometimes complex task that the
abductee must do, even though he might not consciously know how
to perform it. Usually the abductee finds that he can, in fact,
do the job. Once again, asking the key question of what the .
beings are doing while the task is being performed or right

afterwards is critical.

THE ROLE OF CATHARSIS

One of the central problems with the abduction phenomenon is
that while it is occurring, the abductees are not able to vent
their normal emotions. Furthermore, sometimes extraordinarily
traumatic events may be “locked up” inside the abductee, unable

il

to come out and be worked through emotionally. One of the
functions of hypnosis is to allow the abductee to react with a
normal range of emotions to the material as it is remembered 50
that he can begin to understand and emotionally come to terms
with his predicament. Another function is to bring the events
forward into normal memory 50 that they can be discussed,
confronted, and ultimately forgotten in a normal manner.

Although the cathartic effect of recall can be great, it is not a
substitute for support and counseling which must take place along
with the catharsis. Jacobs and Hopkins work towards helping
abductees to obtain emotional and intellectual control over their
situations, but, unfortunately, physical control cannot be
gained. When an abductee ceases to worry about the abductions as
much as before, begins to put the pieces of the puzzle of his
life together, and wants to get on with his life even though he
knows that he might be abducted again, a victory has been won for
the abductee and the therapist. This can happen early on, within
five or six sessions, or it can happen after many sessions.
Unfortunately, for some people it can never be achieved.

Throughout the course of sessions it is helpful that the
abductee is allowed the opportunity to meet and interact with
ether abductees who have a similar level of knowledge about the
phenomenon. Support, groups serve to allow the abductee to
interact with others’ who have had similar experiences and help to
relieve the sense of isolation that often results from the .-
abductions. However, caution must be exercised when the
abductees talk about their experiences. The accounts of

“veterans” might frighten abductees who have just begun to

remember their experiences.

A

DISSOCIATIVE STATES AS A RESULT OF HYPNOSIS.

One must be very careful about guiding a subject: into a
dissociative state in which “channeled” messages come out. In
our opinion, this type of information has no value in abduction
research and serves only to confuse both the therapist and the’

subject.

A few researchers are in the habit of requiring the
abductee, during an hypnotic session, to ask the aliens a
question even though the abduction event was obviously in the
‘past and no such question was asked at the time. Some abductees
will duly ask the question and receive an "answer" in the present
tense. Sometimes the subject will even adopt another voice in
which to relay the message. The hypnotist has put the person
into an unwitting dissociative state closely akin to
“channeling.” Like “past life regressions,” these techniques do
12

the abductee a disservice by clouding the issue with internally
generated material, therefore preventing him from realistically
confronting the serious problems engendered from the abduction
phenomenon, It is extremely important for the
researcher/therapist to recognize these dissociative states and
to understand that they may have nothing to do with the abduction

phenomenon.

CONFABULATION

. Abductees will often confabulate experiences and it is
essential for the therapist or researcher to be aware of this.

The mental and perceptual changes that are induced in an abductee
at the start of the abduction are primarily responsible for
confabulation. Asking purposely wrong leading questions is
helpful in discerning confabulation, especially in initial

sessions.

Confabulation can occur at any time, but there are three
characteristics areas which must be carefully monitored. The
first area is the physical description of aliens. Many
researchers ask the’abductee immediately after the abduction
begins to describe the aliens. This might be perfectly
appropriate, but in general it is better to wait until the
abductee is well into describing later events. The situation is
50 anxiety-ridden and so frightening that often abductees will
confabulate what the aliens look like. Instead, have the
abductees describe the aliens when the situation organically
calls for it, for example after Mindscan or the examination.
casual in your questioning, asking about the appearance of the
being’s nose, eyes, mouth, hair, chin, and so forth. The
information gathered will be more accurate in the middle or end

of an abduction than at the beginning.

Be

The second area of confabulation occurs in the recounting of
alien conversation. Most abductees say that the communication
they receive is in the form of impressions that they
automatically translate into words. It is quite easy to
mistakenly translate one’s own impressions into words and assume
they are coming from the aliens. This does not mean that
everything that is said should be discounted, but it does mean
that extreme caution should be exercised.

The third area of confabulation occurs when the abductee is
asked to interpret alien motivations, intentions, and purposes.
Basically, most abductees do not know what the various procedures
mean and they usually know even less about alien intentions.
13

Asking abductees to relate why something is happening very often
results in material that is not reliable. Therefore, the answer
to any why questions should be treated with suspicion.

Confabulation will often come with perceptual difficulties.
It is a good idea to check on the consistency of information when
possible during the course of the abduction by re-asking similar
questions when appropriate. Finally, confabulation will occur as
a function of wish fulfillment. The abductee does not want to be
abducted so he will say, for example, that he never left his
home. Careful questioning will often reveal a different scenario

that the abductee will acknowledge is correct.

THE CONSEQUENCES OF KNOWLEDGE

Uncovering memories of abductions can create tensions within
the family situation. The abductee often comes with a spouse to
the sessions and the two of them learn about the phenomenon
together. At times a spouse who is not an abductee might feel
frightened about the abductions and that fear might extend to not
wanting to be in the same room with the abductee at night. This
is especially true of non-married couples. At other times, the
spouse refuses to believe that the phenomenon is happening and
therefore cannot give the emotional support that the abductee
requires. Marital tensions can easily arise as a result of this.

Often the revelation of knowledge about abductions can allow
a person to understand why he or she has thought and acted in
what was previously thought of as puzzling ways. Abductees often
realize that their previously inexplicable thoughts and actions
were entirely appropriate to the situation as they encountered
it. Furthermore, the procedures administered to them might have
profoundly affected their self-esteem, and therefore their
relationships. Competent research and therapy can quickly change
a person in very positive ways, raising ego-strength’and self~
esteem.- However, this change can create new problems because it
can happen so quickly that the spouse either has trouble keeping
pace with the changes psychologically or has little desire to
accommodate himself to the new situation: In a manner of
speaking, one suddenly finds oneself married to a different
person. The therapist should anticipate these problems and work
with the couple to facilitate a smooth transition to the new

reality.
14

CHILDREN

Our research has strongly indicated that the abduction
phenomenon is intergenerational. Adults who have been abducted
often have children who are also abducted. As a rule, the
authors do not recommend hypnosis of children and adolescents.
The consequences of the revelation of the abduction phenomenon on
children is not known. If the child thinks that he is actually
being abducted, he might think that he is in some way "special,"
a situation that could lead to a cutting off of ties with his
peers. Or he might think that he in some way "caused" this to
‘happen not only ‘to him but to his family, and it is therefore a
deserved punishment for him. These thoughts can lead to
emotional isolation for the child and can have deleterious
effects on his emotional well-being and development.

If the parents insist that what is happening to the child is
only a dream, then tensions can arise. The “dreams” are so
lifelike that the child might think that there is something very
wrong with thinking that they are real. Furthermore, if he knows
that they are not dreams, the child can become suspicious of his
parents’ judgement and their ability to help him. Therefore it
is important to allow the child to have his own.perceptions of
what is happening té him and to ventilate his feelings about it.
Believing the child’s account of his experiences is important.
Assuring the child that he is loved and cared for and saying that
there are some things from which the parents cannot protect him
might also be useful. Above all it is critical that the child’s
parents fully understand the abduction phenomenon so that they
can be sensitive and understanding about the child’s needs.

Unfortunately, many of the abduction procedures experienced
by children are much the same as adults, and psychological damage
might be occurring as a result. The problem is compounded when
during an abduction the child sees a helpless parent who cannot
protect him. This can lead to unconscious feelings of anger and
mistrust toward the parent. Conversely, parents often have
overwhelming feelings of guilt when they cannot protect their
children. Ultimately it is the responsibility of the parents to
Guage when the child is mature enough to begin to come to terms
with the abduction events.

‘The use of the Hopkins Image Recognition Test can be an

effective diagnostic tool for the parents and it is also an
excellent means of opening communication with younger children.

CONCLUSION

Researching abductions and therapeutically treating
abductees is new and unprecedented. Standardization of
methodology in both areas is just beginning. In the meantime,
sensitive and caring trial and error is our most important
teacher. It is essential to share information not only about
what constitutes the abduction phenomenon, but also about our
successes and failures in learning about its effects on the
victims. The authors welcome all suggestions for useful
investigative and support methodologies that researchers and
therapists have devised in this most difficult and perplexing

field.

SOVIET UFO SECRETS

The breakup of the former Soviet Union opens up a hidden vault of Iron Curtain UFO files.

A Special Exclusive Report by Bryan Gresh

oO meeting was ever complete without food.
N Whether tea and “special occasion” cookies

offered by a government biologist who stud-
ies reported UFO landing sites, or the homemade cab-
bage pie served by a retired Red Army colonel who
ran an unprecedented ten year study of UFOs in the
former Soviet Union, we never left an interview hun-
gry. Our Russian hosts were warm, sincere, friendly
and, despite fixed incomes that amounted to little
more than 25 or 30 US dollars a month, happy to
share a meal with their American visitors. They also
shared with us the secrets of decades of study into the
UFO phenomenon in the vast Soviet empire.

During our 10-day Russian stay in March of this
year, colleague George Knapp and I met, and conduct-
ed lengthy interviews with, a previously hidden eche-
lon of researchers and scientists, dedicated profession-
als who had pursued their interest in UFOs during the
long, dark reign of the Comnuunists. Their findings
were not known to the outside world—until now,
Among our contacts: one of the architects of the
Russian “Star Wars" program who emphatically
claims the exchange of information between aliens
and humans was incorporated in Russian “beam
weapon” technology: a Moscow professor who his
spent 30 years studying reported UFO landing sites,
including many in and around Moscow; a Russian sci-
entist with first-hand knowledge of the reported crash
ofa UFO near Roswell, New Mexico in 1947, a quiet,
shadowy figure who heads the ongoing Ministry of
Defense studies into UFOs: and an author and seien-
list who becume, in essence, a non-person for talking
publicly about the UFO phenomenon.

The trip to Russia took seven months to set up and
was arranged by our man in Moscow, Nikolai
Kapranov. Kapranoy is a Russian physicist who
served as Security Advisor to the Soviet Parliament.
We met Kapranov during a lecture tour of the United
States. The UFO subject, it seems, had been talked
about for years among Kapranov's circle of friends
und associates, including scientists, military officers
and KGB operatives. We set Kapranov up in Moscow
with an office and a salary and asked him to see whit
hard information he could find.

What we had hoped, as odd as it sounds, was to
find out a little of what America knows about UFOs
by talking to the Russians. It has long been assumed if
the US government were secretly monitoring UFOs,
the same might be going on in the Soviet Union. If the
American government had been stockpiling this infor-

subject of UFOs. Maybe the veil of secrecy would be
lifted, if only to offer a peek. But it wasn’t going to be
easy.

When we stepped off the plune at Moscow's airport

Russia’s ongoing political turmoil was as much on our

On our last day in Moscow, the Russian Parliament

Square. Despite not knowing how, or when, our visit

the lure was too great.

One of the bigger gems was Boris Sokolov, a

entire Soviet Union became one gigantic UFO listen-
ing post.”

The year was 1980. An amazing order came down
from the Ministry of Defense: Every military unit in
the vast Soviet empire was to report any and all UFO
sightings. Pilots, soldiers and sailors were ordered to

With the exception of the original “engage” order,
Sokolov says the Soviets adapted a passive observa-
tion stance — if they saw a UFO, fine. The Soviet
attitude was unlike the Americans, Sokolov says, who
had set up some 30 radar stations to track UFOs,

okolov says the military was interested in UFOs

for a number of reasons, including the belief if
the secrets of the UFO could be discovered, the
Soviets would be able to win the competition against
prospective enemies by incorporating what extrater-
restrials knew of velocity, materials — and stealth.
Sokolov told us the search for-stealth technology was
prompted by the capabilities of UFOs.

October 5, 1983 is a date Sokolov will long remem-
ber. He received an order from his commander to Icave
immediately for an ICBM base in the Ukraine. The rea-
son for the urgency? A report from the base commander
to the Chief of the General Staff that the day before.
from 4 until 8 that evening, a UFO had been observed
near the base. During that time, the lights had {fit up on
the base control pane! — the launch codes for the
ICBM's had, mysteriously, been enabled.

“They received an order to prepare the launch of
the ICBM’s,” said Sokolov. “The chief of the General
Staff wasted no time in sending in our UFO experts.”

Fortunately, no missiles were launched. But in an
article six years later in an issue of the magazine
Soviet Military Review, Soviet military leaders made a
strong case for exchanging UFO information, on a
regular basis, with the West. It was suggested if the

Protesting crowds gather in Kremtin Square.

two sides did not communicate on this issue, a UFO
might someday trigger a nuclear exchange between
ithe superpowers. The Soviet authors admitted UFOs
were tracked by Soviet defense systems and suggested
the same must be true in the United States.

Sokolov reports a number of cases invalved mili-
lary sentinels stationed through the Soviet Union who
shot at approaching UFOs. He says when the guards
started shooting, the UFO would take off.

Thousands of cases came to Sokolov during the
1980's. From his voluminous files, he’s compiled the
400 most intriguing cases. Amazingly, we were
allowed to purchase the entire file, and are now in the

_ process of having the documents translated. From the

archives, Sokolov says, it can be determined that an
overwhelming number of the military sightings can be
explained in conventional terms. Stil, many remain a
mystery. Says Sokoloy, “It's a phenomena that cannot
be explained away so far.” ‘

Sokolov is quick to point out that, during the entire
length of the study, not one piece of material evidence
was left behind. However, Sokolov is aware of one
piece of recovered, although unidentified, material
which has been sent to four different Russian insti-
tutes for.analysis. Al! four centers agree, the elements
which form the material cannot be contbined on earth.

MUFON UFO JOURNAL

Though a modest man, Sokolov admits no one else
in the former Soviet Union has more information on
UFOs than himself. As for the aging colonel, he is
quite certain the UFO phenomenon is for real, and is
convinced we will someday find the physical evidence
to prove it. “Our world is not unique,” he says, “it can
be imagined.”

imili Avramenko’s world is somewhat unique. He
R is one of the chief scientists working on Russia's
version of SDI. Avramenko has been entrusted with the
highest possible clearances. So high that, for much of his
life, he hasn’t been able to use his real name. If he
wanted to publish something, he would have to use an
alias. We began our interview of this highly-regarded
scientist with what we thought would be a good ice-
breaker: Is the UFO phenomenon for real? The question
was quickly brushed aside, in no uncertain terms.

“My colleagues and I don’t even think that’s a
question!” he bellowed. “Of course they are real!”

The exchange of information between aliens and
humans, the scientist claims, has led to the develop-
ment of what he referred to as the “weapon of the
aliens,” the plasma beam. The space age weaponry
was incorporated into the Soviet version of SDI. Dr.
Avramenko also confirmed for us that Russians knew
UFOs were from somewhere else as early as 1959,
The Americans knew that too, he sajd, because both
sides had the same type of satellite defense warning
systems,

Dr. Avcamenko shared with us a couple of other
startling pronouncements: during the Vietnam War, he
said, a massive UFO flew over Hanoi. Although every
major weapon in that city had its sights set on the
craft, it didn’t budge, Dr. Avramenko also slipped up
and told us the only craft which can approach the
speed of UFO is the American “Aurora” which is
being Mlown in Nevada. When the look of amazement
registered on our faces, Avramenko quickly back-
tracked and said his information was based solely on
articles in the popular press.

Both Sokolov and Avramenko admitted to us mili-
tary studies of UFOs are still underway in Russia.
Some of the still-classified documents we were able to
get out of Russia bear that out. The documents make it
clear the Russian military maintains an ongoing, high
level interest in the UFO subject. The documents
reveal the Russians are far beyond the “Are they
real?” question, They not only know they’re real, the
Russians are trying to find out how to take advantage,
technologically, of that fact. The documents also show
the Russians are monitoring ufology on a worldwide
basis. We have found references to MJ-12. Stull other
American references in the files have never been
mude public in the West.

from the Ministry of Defense official who is in charge

as saying UFOs are real, they fly at incredible speeds
and that he would tell more about what he had seen in

documents also provide information on American
space encounters, including several references to
things seen on the moon by our astronauts, and how
that information was removed from NASA's public
files.

What do the Russians know about the most cele-
brated of all UFO crashes, the July, 1947 report of a
crashed disc outside Roswell, New Mexico? If the
incident were “real,” the Russians should have known
about it, Afler ull, atomic bomb research was going on
in the New Mexicin desert at the time. Could Russian
spies be far away? Our next interview made it seem
apparent that, even in the 40's, the Russians weren’t
buying the weather balloon explanation being prof-
fered to the American people at the time by the US
imnititary,

That interview was with Valeriy Burdakov, a man
who, to my knowledge, has never granted an inter-
view to a Western journalist. In the 1950's, Burdakov
was a scientist at the prestigious Moscow Aviation
Institute, birthplace -of the Soviet space program.
Burdakov’s interest in UFOs led to lecturing on the
subject, lectures which came to the attention of Sergei
Korolyoy, the dean of Soviet rocketry and the founder
of the Russian space program. But- Korolyov did not
admonish the younger Burdakov, instead he confided
in him,

s the now-60-something Burdakov relates, Joseph

Stalin invited Korolyov to a meeting in 1948. The
dictator brought Korolyov to a room where, spread out
on a table, were piles of material and information col-
lected during a top secret study. Some of the information
was gleaned trom reports of Soviet operatives in place in
New Mexico at the time of the alleged crash. Stalin
was anxious to know, what did Korolyov make of this
reported crash of a UFO near Roswell?

MUFON UFO JOURNAL

“Korolyov said, ‘O.K., I'll collect the materials and
bring them back in two days,”” said Burdakov. “But
Stalin told him, ‘No, you can’t take any of this."

Burdakov says Stalin instead ordered Korolyov to
work in the room for as long as he needed, Stalin
offered to supply translators and anything else
Korolyov needed to get the job done. When he fin-
ished, Stalin asked Korolyov for his opinion.

“Korolyov told Stalin the phenomenon was real.”
said Burdakov. “He told him the UFOs were not dan-
gerous to our country, but they were not manufactured
in the United States, or any other country, Stalin
thanked him, and told Korolyov his opinion was
shared by a number of other specialists.”

Our interview with Burdakov revealed other first
hand accounts from the annals of Soviet ufology.
Burdakov says the founding father of the Soviet UFO
movement, Felix Zigel, was asked to lecture the KGB on
the UFO phenomenon, Burdakov says he, too, found
himself on the secret speaking tour, appearing before the
Atomic Energy Institute and other “closed secret insti-
tutes” to share what he knew about UFOs. Burdakoy
says he was guaranteed his lectures would be kept quict.

Burdakov says he has no doubts about the extent of
the American government's involvement in the UFO
phenomenon, Several branches of the American military,
he says, are involved in active research and study,

“We know that the United States Air Force possesses
plenty of material,” says Burdakov. “The U.S. Navy
has a big amount of material as well, We know that
special orders have been given to keep all materials se-
cret. When curious people ask for the materials they ure
told they're not there, that they've been destroyed.”

The study of UFOs in the Soviet Union didn't
always enjoy an unobstructed field of view. Several
times, Soviel wologists found themselves on the outs
with the Soviet hierarchy. Even before Zigel, there
was Yuri Fomin.

Fomin’s interest in UFO dates back to the early 50's
while connected with a Moscow institute working on in-
dustrial/electronic techniques. During lectures he de-
tivered to the Soviet military, he became intrigued by the
number of audience members who were witnesses to
UFO events. His interest, though, was not something the
Soviet system thought he should publicly share. Whether
out of jealousy or fear, the Soviets ordered Fomin to put
a lid on it. Despite a very public attack in Pravda,
Fomin refused. It cost him, Fomin would become a
non-person for talking openly about the subject. Sull, he
would not give up. During those dark days, Soviet ufol-
ogy was driven underground. Yet despite public censure,
Fomin and Zigel were asked to deliver classified lectures
to government and military officials on the very subject
they were being persecuted for talking about publicly.

al, private studies of UFOs since the 1970's. Over the
years, as many as ten different institutions have been

Rem Varlamov of the Moscow Technology Institute
believes evidence abounds. We met with Dr. Varlamov
at his small apartment outside Moscow for an in-depth
interview. Dr, Varlamov has traveled to a number of
reported UFO landing sites in Russia, including ten in
the direct vicinity of Moscow, to conduct a variety of
tests on physical and chemical changes in the sotl and
the environment. Among his voluminous findings:
Identical quart timepivces, one placed inside a land-
ing site, the other just outside, record time at two dif
ferent speeds. The quartz timepiece inside the landing
circle speeds up. Dr. Varlamov has also discovered
that inside the reported landing site circles the land is
all but sterile, yielding just a few individual samples
of single-celled animals per cubic centimeter of soil.
Just outside the circles, tens of thousands of single cell
animals flourish in every cubic centimeter.

Varlamov told us the mitin thrust of his work is not to
contact “the space intellect.” but, rather, to understand
how the universe works. Among his other fascinating
findings: evidence of what is known as “angel hair,” a
type of by-product of UFO exhaust. Varlamov has ob-
tained a sample to analyze the content. Dr. Varlamov
also claims at least six attempts have been made to
make pre-arranged contact with alien intelligence. Some
of these contacts, he claims, were successful.

Varlamov’s findings are supported by a government
biologist and close colleague. His name is Yuri

Simakoy. Simakov has not been known outside of
Russia until now. Dr. Simakov has also traveled to a
number of Russian landing site cases, gathering data
and conducting numerous tests. One of those experi-
ments involved flies.

“We placed flies in a dish and put the dish over a
landing site,” Dr. Simakov said. “The flies went wild,
buzzing and flying around.”

Most intriguing, however, was Simakov’s discovery
of tiny, glass-like balls recovered from soil inside the
landing site circles. Russian scientists, as yet, have not
been able to identify these microscopic marbles.
Simakov has a couple of theories.

“They may be formed as the result of transmuta-
tion,” he said, “when oxygen was transformed into
sodium under the influence of the UFO.” Or, he says,
they may be containers to carry life from one planet to
another.

Although Dr. Simakov did not have many of these
“seeds,” he did give us some, in hopes American sei-
entists might be able to solve the mystery. A highly
reputable lab in the United States has analyzed the
material. We are not yet in receipt of the written
report, but this much is known: a team of experts
which tested and analyzed the little balls has absolute-
ly no idea what they are. These folks are experts in
plant pathology and micro organisms, they suggested
we next contact a chemist. They say the material
uppears to be similar to glass in composition, and
someone familiar with how glass forms in soil may
provide some insight.

Another of Dr. Simakov's discoveries comes from
two reported landing sites in Siberia. Simakov found
microscopic worms in the soil, Nothing too unusual
there, except these worms are particular to Mexico,
and don’t occur naturally anywhere in the former
Soviet Union,

sizable portion of our tine in Moscow was spent

with a military official whose area of expertise
may offend the sensibilities of mainstream American
ufologists. His research has a distinct, New Age fla-
vor. Though at first gkince, it may be easy to dismiss this
work as so much mumbo jumbo, it is important to re-
member who is behind it: the Russian military machine.
Also important to remember: it is a subject taken very
seriously. Again, because of an agreement to protect
his identity, we cannot divulge his name. However, he is
no lightweight. His boss reports directly to the Russian
version of Colin Powell.

In the West, it would be called a “remote viewing”
program, the ability lo “see” classified materials
locked away in a safe thousands of miles away. But
for the general in charge of this program, it entails
much more.

We were shown films which detailed the training of
Russian soldiers. At the completion of the program,

NUMBER 306

these soldiers would be able to track military targets
without the benefit of any equipment. No radar, no
satellites, no night vision glasses—just their minds.
We were told the success rate hovered around 70%.
Some of what the general is working on has already
been put to use by Russian law enforcement.

For the general and his staff, the connection with
UFOs is one of communication. Several times during
our trip, we heard reference to the “universal brain”
and the “cosmic intellect.” These disciples of ufology
believe it is possible to communicate with alien jntel-
ligence on an ongoing basis. Not just “grays,” but
dimensional, spatial beings that live with, and around,
us on Earth. Yes, it’s “out there"-—but no one snickers
when the subject is talked about it.

he study of UFOs in Russia is huge, serious and on-

going. All the secrets cannot be found our in one
trip. Fortunately, we have been invited back to Russia to
continue our investigation behind what was once an
iron curtain. The Ministry of Defense has invited us to
lake part in i UFO safari to study a high concentration
sighting area in Siberja. We would be not only the only
Westerners, but the only reporters. Unfortunately, the trip
had lo be cancelled for this year, because of a lack of
Ministry of Defense funding. Hopefully, we'll make
the trip next year. In the meantime, our company is in
the process of putting together a series of videotaped
documentaries on the “Best Evidence”— both in the
US, Russia and around the world. The first tapes should
be available in carly 1994,

Perhaps the “hew" Russia will bring new opportu.
nities for American and Russian UFO researchers to
compare notes, and exchange vital information gath-
ered [vom opposite sides of the world. To that end, our
company has signed an agreement with the Russian
Academy of Natural Sciences to establish a joint
American-Russian research institute to foster the
exchange of information between both sides. The
UFO phenomenon is, after all, a subject too important
to be left solely up to governments.

Bryan Gresh is Senior Vice President of Altamira
Communications Group. He spent 15 years as a
television news reporter in a number of markets
around the country. Before joining Altamira in
January of 1992, Bryan was an anchor/reporter at
the CBS affiliate in Las Vegas, KLAS-TV.

It was while in Las Vegas that he began working
with renowned UFO researcher, George Knapp.
Knapp and Gresh have teamed up to produce a
series of videotape documentaries on the UFO
phenomenon, the first of which will be available
later this year.

Department and ex officio Presidium Member, born on 9 Apr 1914

1941-1945 and rejoined the staff 1945-1958 at Leningrad State
University, appointed to the research staff in 1958 at the
Institute of Geology and Geophysics (Novosibirsk) in the
Siberian Department of the USSR Academy of Sciences where he
worked for most of the rest of his career, originally elected
a Corresponding Member in 1958 in the Geology, Geophysics,
Geochemistry and Mining Sciences and Siberian Departments of
the USSR Academy of Sciences, appointed to a senior teaching
post in 1960 at Novosibirsk State University and became a full
Professor there in 1964, elected a Member in 1963 of the
Geological Society of France, 1967 Lenin Prizewinner, elected
a Member in 1968 of the Geological Society of Sweden, elected
an Academician on 26 Nov 1968 in the same Departments of USSR

Experts puzzled by UFO sightings

EXPERTS continue to be baffled by
unusual sightings in the skies of South
Humberside.

Scunthorpe and Glanford's UFO
mystery began last week when a number
of people said they spolted strange
objects in the sky.

Ministry of Defence spokesman con-
firmed that the were no RAF aircraft in
the area al the time. |

And another at RAF Waddington said
they were not flying in the area on the
night most of the sightings were
recorded.

MoD representative Nick Pope said
that while accepting that the majority of
UFO sightings could be explained, they
kept an open mind on those that could
not.

Now the cases are being looked into b
Yorkshire-based British UFO Research
Association spokesman Phillip Mantle.

He said the most remarkable factor
was that ple had said the objects
were soundless.

“Ninety-five per cent of these things
have a rational explanation,” said Mr

Mantle, adaiug that weather phenomena
and aircraft were common causes.

“But there is just that five per cent
that would appear to defy explanation,”
he said.

South Humberside investigator Adrian
Cress of ‘Quest International has
appealed for those who have seen objects
in the sky to contact him.

And an investigation into the cases
reported in last week's Evening Telc-
graph is already under way by BUFORA.

Quest Inlernational can be contacted
on 0756-752216.

|Hinckley area have been

The sightings began at 6pm on
Tuesday 21st September and six
witnesses contacted the group.

They reported a strange disc-
shaped object hovering over the
town towards the north-west which
suddenly vanished after about 10
minutes,

The following afternoon at abou

' 3pm, a dark, silent cigaretic-shapext
object flew slowly over two wil-
nesses in Wood Street, say the
group.

And then, on Thursday 30th
September, a Hinckley man spot-
ted whathedescribedasa “gigantic
ball of light” hovering low above
houses in the Ashby Read area.

According to his statement, the
light faded out after several min-
utes and made no sound.

‘The last sighting to be reported
occurred on Friday IstOctober. At
7.20pm,a young witness claims to
have seea a brightly lit object which
appeared 10 hover over Burbage ut
first and then move down towards
the roottops before fading out.

an unbiascd organisation which
investipates LIFO phenomena.

Busy skies boost

UFO activity over Waltham
Forest is rife according to
the head of London’s main
paranormal studies group.

In fact Roy Lake, chairman of Barking-
based London UFO Studies, says there is
such interest over UFOs in the borough
that his meetings held in Walthamstow
are usually packed out.

The next one which takes place at
Unity Hall, Bramley Close, Waltham-
stow, on Friday at 7.30pm should be no
exception.

Roy says several peaple are attending
who have reported seeing UFOs over
Chingford Reservoirs over recent months

THE RAF and police have been un-
able to shed any light on a ‘UFO
sighting over Canterbury.

The unidentified flying object was seen hover-

speed towards Ashford.

was spoucd by a motorist and her passenger, trav-
elling slong the London-bound A2 towards Canter-
bury. She alto reported seeing a military heticopter
thousands of fect below the object.

Baffled by the sight of a large, bright, cigar-shaped
object in the night sky, they were even more surpi
when a strong ray of light shone from it and it disap-
peared — only to reappear seconds later in the area of
Ashford.

“Iu seemed to have several tources of light shining
from it, and was motionless when we first saw it,” said
driver Carol Davies, from Nelson Rosd, Gillingham.

“At firm 1 thought it might be part of some kind of

“When the beam of light shone out and it disap-

By Caroline Evans

Wales on Sunday - Cardiff

VE GLT aes

THE Welsh UFO mystery may have been solved by a
West Wales country club.

For the suspect extra-terrestrials may be 4 revolutionary new light
show at the Gwemllwyn Country Club at Cross Hands in Dyfed.

Last month Wales on Sunday spoke' to terrified families who
claimed they had been tracked by strange lights for miles across the
Welsh countryside.

Aman from Leominster, who was driving from Fishguard back to
England along the M4, claimed his car was followed for five miles by
@ pulsating cloud.

“Tt was really eerle. There appeared to be a cloud about 30ft long
and 12f, to the right of the car which seemed to be pulsating with
lights,” said Brian Faulkner.

“It followed us for about five miles and was vibrating as if it was
breathing and ready to attack. I saw it break up, the lights just broke
up and shot off to my left at a steep angle away from us. I have never
believed in UFOs, but I would like to know what that was,” he said.

Now Martin Edwards, operations director of Llanelli-based PA Ins-
tallations, believes he may have the key to the mystery, a high-
powered spotlight called the Space Flower.

He imported the light from Italy and installed it at the Gwemlwyn
Country Club at Cross Hands as an advertising gimmick.

“You can see it from about 12 miles away on a clear night. On a
cloudy night it reflects of the base of the clouds, it looks quite spooky
because it doesn’t look as if it is coming from the ground,” said Mr

“It is in common use in America and Europe to promote discos.
The idea is that people can follow the light to the club,” he said.

But Mr Faulkner says that If the mystery lights he saw were from
the club they should be stopped because he believes they could cause
an accident.
tng ears People and for 8 woman on her own ft would De terry

” he said. :

Mr Edwards dismissed the idea that the light is a hazard: “We
have been given clearance by the Civil Aviation Authority and as far
as accidents are concemed, someone walking on the hard shoulder
can be distracting,” he sald.

Last month two Carmarthen women and their children were terri
fled when they spotted a dise-shaped light in the sky which they say
followed thern along the same route for 20 miles. :

‘The light they saw was a bright soft white colour and It appeared
to single out the children in the back of the car shining directly on
them at one point before shooting off The following day"they all suf-
fered Various symptoms including ear problems and rashes: :

Lorraine Fox and Diane Jones would like to believe that what they
saw was the light from the Cross Hands club, but the light which fol-
lowed them looked different, they said. .

A mother and her 20-year-old
daughter this week claimed to have
been frightened after a strange silent
craft followed them down Green Road.

Their encounter lasted about an hour and a
half. The two, who live in the Derby Road’ area
of Ashbourne, became aware of an clongated H
shape in the sky on their right as they passed the
Little Chef travelling the AS2 towards
Ashbourne, It was about 9.10pm.

“Tt was not a plane or a helicopter and had
lights at the bottom like strobe lights moving
around very slowly,” recalled the mother, “St
was square shape and very large in the sky,

“l wanted to go home when we got to
Ashbourne but my daughter said to go to Green
Road, where [ knew that we should see it.

“Against my better judgment we went
although I was frightened because I knew it
would be there.

“Just past the Grammar School my daughter
shouted that it was there on the left above the
houses,

“Thad already locked the car doors and drove
up Green Road to about ten houses before the
end, and stopped.

“Now it seemed to have lost shape and was
replaced by a. bright flashing star which we felt
compelled to focus on.

“We were quiet, and kept looking for about
five minutes and then suddenly there it was
above us.

“E leaned forward and looked through the
windscreen and directly above us was this saucer

shaped thing, as big as a house and as high as a
house.

“Tt was round, and a silvery white colour with
white flashing lights around it and other round
shapes around it like glowing ball lights.

“There was no sound at all and I was petrified
and my daughter screamed ‘Oh my God.” I felt
as though it could have beamed us up, so put the
car in reverse and backed down Green Road at
about 40 miles an hour.

“As I did so my daughter was shouting that it
was following us, but by the lime we got near to
Town Head it had gone over the houses and
trees and { felt a bit safer and stopped again.”

They both got out of the car and stood
Jooking up into the sky when they again saw the
elongated H shape which, as they looked,
changed into a cigar shape.

“As we looked it took off and shot across to
the left, back to the right and back to the left.
Then it disappeared.”

The two now decided to go home but getting
back in the car found it was icy cold, as though a
frosty morning, although the heater had been on
all the way from Derby.

Driving up Derby Road and looking across

the valley they saw the H shape still over Green
Road and arriving home the woman got her

They followed the strange craft up to

“My husband works in the aerospace

The woman admitted she had been very

“Although | am pleased ta have seen it and

to disappear and me with it.”

In July when a woman on the Green Road
area was also frightened by a strange huge disc
shaped object, several News Telegraph readers
suggested it was a Chinook helicopter.

But when the News Telegraph described the
experience and description to RAF Strike
Command at High Wycombe, spokesman Dale
Donovan said it did not sound at all like a
helicopter.

“The Chinook, while being 99 fect jong
including the rotar at each end, is a very loud
acroplane making a very distinctive vibrating
noise.

“It also has a considerable down-draught.

“I think you, are talking here of something
more than a helicopter.”

At the Ministry of Defence in Whitehall,
UFO sightings are dealt with by Mr Nick Pope
who has asked that the women in question should
send him full details of the incident and a sketch
of the craft.

“Ninety-five per cent of reports we receive
can be explained in terms of known objects or
phenomena,” explained Mr Pope.

“Satellites, aircraft, weather balloons,
meteorites and so on. But we accept five per cent
of sightings do appear to defy explanation and on
which we are open minded and have no better
idea than the next person,”

Last year Mr Pope received 147 reports from
members of the public, usually via the local
police,

So far he has received no reports for Tuesday
evening last week.
